###### **Device Reconfiguration Commands** 

###### **SET COUNTER** 

###### **Description** 

Counters can be a real counter or a variable. This setting sets the counter number in the program and its increments. There are three different types of counters: digit (0~9~0), lower case letter (a~z~a) or upper case letter (A~Z~A). 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET COUNTER @n step** 

**@n=** " **Expression"** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|@n|n: counter number. There are 61 counters available (@0 ~ @60) in the printer. @0<br>to @50 will be cleared while restarting the printer. @51 to @60 will be stored in<br>printer until the printer is restored to factory default.<br>**_@51~@55 were supported since V6.37 EZ._**<br>**_@56~@60 were supported since V6.74 EZ._**|
|Step|The increment of the counter, can be positive or negative.<br>-999999999 <= step <= 999999999|
||_If the counter is used as a fixed variable, please set the increment to 0._|
|Expression|Initial string. String length is 101 bytes|



**<mark>269</mark>** 



<!-- Start of picture text -->
Label 5 ee.<br>0005 0101 999<br>Label 4 —-555-————-5i5]----ga6<br><!-- End of picture text -->

<mark>||</mark> 

**SET CUTTER** 

###### **Description** 

This setting activates or deactivates the cutter and defines how many printed labels is to be cut at one time. This setting will be saved in printer memory after turning off the power. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET CUTTER OFF/BATCH/pieces** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|Disable cutter function.|
|BATCH|Set printer to cut label at the end of printing job.|
|Pieces|Set number of printing labels per cut. 0<= pieces <=65535|



###### **_Note:_** 

| **_Care label cutter module was supported since V6.86 EZ in industrial printer TTP-2410M series._**|
|---|



 **_Since V6.86 EZ, if cutter is not installed, the cutter error doesn’t happen even SET CUTTER ON is set._** 

###### **Example** 

|**Sample code**|**Result**|
|---|---|
|**SIZE 3,3**<br>**GAP 0,0**||
|**SET CUTTER OFF**||
|**SET PEEL OFF**<br>**CLS**|**The cutter function is disabling.**|
|**TEXT 50,50,**"**3",0,1,1, "SET CUTTER OFF"**<br>**PRINT 3**||
|**SET CUTTER BATCH**||
|**CLS**||
|**TEXT 50,50,**"**3",0,1,1, "SET CUTTER BATCH"**<br>**PRINT 3,2**|**The cutter cuts once after 6 labels are printed.**|



**<mark>271</mark>** 

|**SET CUTTER 1**<br>**CLS**<br>**TEXT 50,50,**"**3",0,1,1,**"**SET CUTTER 1"**<br>**PRINT 3,2**|**The cutter cuts every label.**|
|---|---|
|**CLS**||
|**TEXT 50,50,**"**3",0,1,1,**"**SET CUTTER 2"**<br>**PRINT 3,2**|**The cutter cuts every 2 labels.**|



###### **See Also** 

OFFSET, PRINT, SET PARTIAL_CUTTER 

**<mark>272</mark>** 

###### **SET PARTIAL_CUTTER** 

###### **Description** 

This setting activates or deactivates the cutter and defines how many printed labels is to be cut at one time. This setting will be saved in printer memory after turning off the power. This function prevents label back feeding after a cut. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET PARTIAL_CUTTER OFF/BATCH/Pieces** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|Disable cutter function.|
|BATCH|Set printer to cut label at the end of printing job.|
|Pieces|Set number of printing labels per cut. 0<= pieces <=65535|
|**Note: This comman**|**d is supported for the printer that have cutter module.**|



###### **Example** 

###### **Sample code** 

**REM **SET PARTIAL_CUTTER FUNCTION OFF EXAMPLE PROGRAM** SIZE 3,1 GAP 0,0 DENSITY 8 SPEED 6 DIRECTION 0 REFERENCE 0,0 SET PARTIAL_CUTTER OFF CLS TEXT 50,50, "3",0,1,1, "SET PARTIAL_CUTTER OFF" PRINT 3 REM ***This program cuts once at the batch*** SET PARTIAL_CUTTER BATCH CLS TEXT 50,50, "3",0,1,1, "SET PARTIAL_CUTTER BATCH" PRINT 3,2 REM ***This program cuts every label***** 

**<mark>273</mark>** 

**SET PARTIAL_CUTTER 1** 

**CLS TEXT 50,50,** " **3",0,1,1,** " **SET PARTIAL_CUTTER 1" PRINT 3,2 REM ***This program cuts 2 label*** SET PARTIAL_CUTTER 2 CLS TEXT 50,50, "3",0,1,1, "SET PARTIAL_CUTTER 2" PRINT 3,2** 

###### **See Also** 

OFFSET, PRINT, SET CUTTER 

**<mark>274</mark>** 

**SET BACK** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is used after SET CUTTER function. This function prevents label backfeeding after a cut. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET BACK OFF/ON/ SUPRESS** 

|**Parameter**<br>**Description**|
|---|
|OFF<br>Disable back function.|
|ON<br>Enable back function. Additional length of the label will be printed to ensure<br>the content is complete.|
|SUPRESS<br>Disable back function. To adhere to the current SIZE setting for label printing,<br>some parts of the content may be missing. (Since A2.17)|
|Note: TDP-643 Plus, TTP-243, TTP-342, TTP-244ME, TTP-342M and TTP-248M series are not supported this feature.|



###### **Example** 

**<mark>Sample code</mark> REM **SET BACK FUNCTION OFF EXAMPLE PROGRAM** SIZE 3,1 GAP 0,0 DENSITY 8 SPEED 6 DIRECTION 1 REFERENCE 0,0 SET CUTTER 1 SET BACK OFF CLS TEXT 50,50,** " **3",0,1,1,** " **SET BACK OFF" PRINT 3 CLS SET CUTTER 1 SET BACK ON TEXT 50,50,** " **3",0,1,1,** " **SET BACK ON" PRINT 3** 

###### **See Also** 

OFFSET, PRINT, SET CUTTER 

**<mark>275</mark>** 

**SET KEYn** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is used to enable/disable the KEYn function. Before setting KEYn function, please disable the default function of KEYn first. The setting will remain resident in the printer even when the printer is power off. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET KEYn ON/OFF/DEFAULT/MENU/PAUSE/PRINT m/FEED/BACKFEED/FORMFEED/CUT/INPUT "string"** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|n|0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6|
|ON|Enable KEYn function|
|OFF|Disable KEYn function|
|DEFAULT|Set KEYn back to default function|
|MENU|Set to "MENU" key|
|PAUSE|Set to "PAUSE" key|
|PRINT m|Set to "PRINT" key<br>m: Set number of printing labels per print. (0 < m < 32000)|
|FEED|Set to "FEED" key that that can manual control the feeding distance by keep<br>pressing the key.|
|BACKFEED|Set to "BACKFEED" key that that can manual control the back feeding distance by<br>keep pressing the key.|
|FORMFEED|Set to "FORMFEED" key that will feed the label under the format. Ex: If format is<br>"size 4,6, it will feed 6".|
|CUT|Set to "CUT" key|
|INPUT "string"|Send the command by press key (ex:**SET KEY1 INPUT "CONFIG" + CHR$(13) +**<br>**CHR$(10)**)|



The default function of KEYn id as listed below: 

|**Model**|**KEY0**|**KEY1**|**KEY2**|**KEY3**|**KEY4**|**KEY5**|**KEY6**|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|TDP-643 Plus/ 643R Plus||PAUSE||||||
|TTP-243/243 Plus/243 Pro series, TTP-<br>244ME/244 ME Plus/244M Pro series,<br>TTP-244/ 244 Plus series||PAUSE|FEED|||||
|TDP-245/247 series, TTP-245/247 series,<br>TTP-245C series, TDP-225 series, TTP-225<br>series, TA200 series, Alpha-3R, DA series,<br>TE series, Alpha-2R, TDM-20, TDM-30,<br>Alpha-30R||FEED||||||



**<mark>276</mark>** 

|TX200/210 series (with LCD), TC210 series<br>(with LCD), MX240P series, MX241 series,<br>MH series, ML240P series, ML241 series,<br>MB240T/241T series, PEX series, MH261<br>series, Alpha-30L/40L series,||FEED|MENU|UP|RIGHT|LEFT|DOWN|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|TTP-246M series||MENU|PAUSE|FEED|(UP)|(DOWN)|(SELECT)|
|TTP-248M series||MENU|PAUSE|FEED||||
|TTP-2410M/2410M Pro series, TTP-246M<br>Plus/246M Pro series, TTP-268M series,<br>TTP-384M series, ME240(with LCD) series||MENU|PAUSE|FEED|UP|DOWN|SELECT|
|ME240 (Non-LCD) series, ML240 series,<br>MB240 series||FEED|PAUSE|||||
|M23 series|FEED|LEFT|MID|RIGHT||||
|Alpha-4L||FEED|INFO|MENU||||
|MX240 series, TTP-2410MT/MU series||PAUSE|MENU|FEED|UP|SELECT|DOWN|
|DH/TH series||FEED|PAUSE|REPRINT||||



**Note: Please refer to printer model list for more detail** 

###### **Example** 

###### **Sample code** 

**DOWNLOAD "DEMO.BAS"** 

**SIZE 3,1** 

**GAP 0,0** 

**DENSITY 8** 

**SPEED 3** 

**DIRECTION 0 REFERENCE 0,0 SET CUTTER OFF SET KEY1 OFF SET KEY2 OFF SET KEY3 OFF KEY1=0 KEY2=0 KEY3=0** 

**:START** 

**IF KEY1=1 THEN** 

**<mark>277</mark>** 

**CLS** 

**TEXT 100,10, "3",0,1,1, "KEY1 (MENU key) is pressed!! "** 

**PRINT 1,1** 

**ELSEIF KEY2=1 THEN CLS TEXT 100,10, "3",0,1,1, "KEY2 (PAUSE key) is pressed!! " PRINT 1,1 ELSEIF KEY3=1 THEN CLS TEXT 100,10, "3",0,1,1, "KEY3 (FEED key) is pressed!! " TEXT 100,60, "3",0,1,1, "End of test" PRINT 1,1** 

**SET KEY1 ON SET KEY2 ON SET KEY3 ON END ENDIF GOTO START EOP DEMO** 

###### **See Also** 

OFFEST, PRINT 

**<mark>278</mark>** 

**SET LEDn** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is used to control LED on/off function. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET LED1 ON/OFF SET LED2 ON/OFF SET LED3 ON/OFF** 

|**LED no.**|**Default Function**|
|---|---|
|LDE1|Power on/off|
|LED2|Printer on-line/off-line|
|LED3|Error/normal|
|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|ON|Enable LED_n_function|
|OFF|Disable LED_n_function|



|The default function of LED1, L|ED2 and|LED3 id|as liste|d below:|||||**LED2 &**|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|**Model**|**LED1**|**LED2**|**LED3**|**LED4**|**LED5**|**LED6**|**LED7**|**LED8**|<br>**LED3**|
|TDP-643 Plus/643R Plus series|ONLINE|ERROR|ERROR|||||||
|TTP-243/243 Plus/243 Pro series,<br>TTP-244ME/244 ME Plus/244M Pro<br>series, TTP-244/ 244 Plus series<br>TTP-2410M/2410M Pro series, TTP-<br>246M Plus/246M Pro series, TTP-<br>268M series, TTP-384M series,<br>ME240 series, MX240 series,<br>MX240P series|POWER|ONLINE|ERROR|||||||
|MB240 series|GREEN|GREEN|RED|CARRIAGE|<br>RIBBON|PAPER|WIRELESS||ORANGE|
|TDP-245/247 series, TTP-245/247<br>series, TTP-245C series, TDP-225<br>series, TTP-225 series, DA200<br>series, TA200 series, TC210series,<br>TE200 series, MH series<br>**Note: For this series, the LED1=LED2**|GREEN|GREEN|RED||||||ORANGE|
|Alpha-2R series, Alpha-3R series,<br>TDM-20 series,TDM-30 series|GREEN|GREEN|RED|BAT1|BAT2|BAT3|BT/WIFI||ORANGE|

|DH/TH series(LED version only)|GREEN|GREEN|RED||RIBBON|PAPER|CARRIAGE|WIRELESS|<br>ORANGE|
|**_Note: Please refer toprinter mod_**|**_el list for_**|**_checking_**|**_series_**|**_printers._**||||||



**<mark>279</mark>** 

###### **Example** 

**<mark>Sample code</mark> DOWNLOAD "DEMO4.BAS" SET LED1 OFF SET LED2 OFF SET LED3 OFF FOR I=1 TO 100 LED1=0 LED2=0 LED3=0 IF I-INT(I/2)*2=0 THEN LED1=1 ELSEIF I-INT(I/3)*3=0 THEN LED2=1 ELSE LED3=1 ENDIF NEXT LED1=1 LED2=1 LED3=0 SET LED1 ON SET LED2 ON SET LED3 ON EOP DEMO4** 

**<mark>280</mark>** 

**SET PEEL** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is used to enable/disable the self-peeling function. The default setting for this function is off. When this function is set on, the printer stops after each label printing, and does not print the next label until the peeled label is taken away. This setting will be saved in printer memory when turning off the power. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET PEEL ON/OFF** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Enable the self-peeling function|
|OFF|Disable the self-peeing function|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code REM ***SELF-PEELING FUNCTION ON*** SIZE 4,4 GAP 0,0 DENSITY 8 SPEED 6 DIRECTION 0 REFERENCE 0,0 SET CUTTER OFF SET PEEL ON CLS TEXT 50,100, "3",0,1,1, "SELF-PEELING FUNCTION TEST" PRINT 5** 

###### **See Also** 

OFFEST, PRINT 

**<mark>281</mark>** 

**SET REWIND** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is used to enable/disable the internal rewind function for MX240/TTP-2610MT series & external rewind module (via RS-232 port). The default setting for this function is off. When this function is set on, the printer rewind spindle will rewind the printed labels. This setting will be saved in printer memory when turning off the power. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET REWIND ON/OFF/RS232** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Enable the internal rewind function|
|OFF|Disable the internal rewind or external rewind module function|
|RS232|Enable the external rewind module function (via RS-232 port/ pull high signal)|
|**_Note: The functio_**|**_n of external rewind module has been supported since A1.92 and later firmware._**|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code REM ***REWIND FUNCTION ON*** SIZE 4,4 GAP 0.12,0 DENSITY 8 SPEED 6 DIRECTION 0 REFERENCE 0,0 SET CUTTER OFF SET REWIND ON CLS TEXT 50,100, "3",0,1,1,** " **REWIND FUNCTION TEST" PRINT 500** 

**<mark>282</mark>** 

###### **See Also** 

OFFEST, PRINT 

**<mark>283</mark>** 

**SET TEAR & SET STRIPER** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to enable/disable feeding of labels to gap/black mark position for tearing off. This setting will be saved in printer memory when turning off the power. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET TEAR ON/OFF (TSPL2 language printers only) SET STRIPER ON/OFF (TSPL language printers only)** 

Note: Please refer to printer model list for checking TSPL or TSPL2. 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|The label gap will stop at the tear off position after print.|
|OFF|The label gap will NOT stop at the tear off position after print. The beginning<br>of label will be aligned to print head.|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code REM ***TEAR FUNCTION ON*** SIZE 3,3 GAP 0.08,0 DENSITY 8 SPEED 4 DIRECTION 0 REFERENCE 0,0 SET CUTTER OFF SET PEEL OFF SET TEAR ON CLS TEXT 50,100, "3",0,1,1, "TEAR FUNCTION TEST" PRINT 1** 

**<mark>284</mark>** 

###### **See Also** 

SET PEEL, SET CUTTER 

**<mark>285</mark>** 

**SET GAP** 

###### **Description** 

This setting sets the gap sensor emission sensitivity. The printer initiates automatic gap sensor calibration when the PAUSE key is held down while powering up. This function may cease to work if the thickness of the backing paper and that of label with backing paper are not of appreciable difference to the sensor, or when there are pre-printed marks or patterns on the label. In such case, users must calibrate the gap sensor manually by this command through trial-and-error method to attain the proper setting. This setting will be saved in printer memory when turning off the power. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET GAP n/AUTO/OFF/0,/REVERSE/OBVERSE** 

|**Parameter**<br>**Description**||||
|---|---|---|---|
|N<br>Gap sensor light e<br>the lowest sensiti|mission strength<br>vity|. Available range i|s listed as below. 0 is|
|AUTO<br>The printer will fe<br>continuous, the pr<br>the label is contin|ed 2 or 3 labels t<br>inter will feed la<br>uous.|o calibrate the ga<br>bel to limit 10~20|p. If the label is<br>inches to confirm if|
|OFF<br>Disable the SET G|AP AUTO functio|n.||
|0,<br>Automatically cali|brate the gap siz|e.||
|REVERSE<br>This function is us<br>the label and whic<br>of the media whic<br>the printable area|ed when the Bla<br>h can’t be detec<br>h can be passed<br>, otherwise it wil|ck Mark is the sep<br>ted by the Black M<br>through by GAP s<br>l be defined to th|aration in the front of<br>ark sensor. The parts<br>ensor are defined to be<br>e GAP of the media.|
|OBVERSE<br>Disable the "SET G|AP REVERSE" fu|nction.||
|Printer model|Gap Sensor Range|Black Mark Sensor<br>Range|SET GAP REVERSE<br>SET GAP OBVERSE<br>SET GAP AUTO|
|TTP-243 series, TTP-244ME series, TDP-643<br>Plus series, TTP-342 series, TTP-342M series|0~15|ON/OFF|**V**|
|TTP-243 Plus series, TTP-244 series, TTP-<br>244ME Plus series, TDP-643R Plus series, TTP-<br>342 Plus series|0~255|ON/OFF|**V**|
|TTP-243 Pro series, TTP-244 Plus series|0~63|ON/OFF||
|TTP-245C series, TTP-225 series, TDP-225<br>series|0~31|0~3|**V**|
|TTP-245/343 series, TDP-245 series, TTP-<br>246M/344M series (non usb)|0~63|0~63|**V**|
|TTP-245 Plus/343 Plus series, TDP-245 Plus<br>series, TTP-247 series, TDP-247 series|0~15|0~15|**V**|
|TTP-246M/344M series (usb)|0~31|0~31|**V**|



**<mark>286</mark>** 

|TTP-246M Plus, TTP-2410M series, TTP-344M<br>Plus series, TTP-346M series, TTP-384M series,<br>TTP-644M series, ME240 series|0~7|0~3|**V**|
|---|---|---|---|
|TTP-2410M Pro series, TDM-20, TDM-30|0~7|0~7|**V**|
|TTP-248M series, M23 series|0~255|0~255|**V**|
|TA200 series, MB240 series, MH240 series|0~15|0~3|**V**|
|Alpha-4L series|0~15|0~7|**V**|



###### **_Note:_** 

**_* When in "SET HEAD OFF" mode, the function "SET GAP AUTO" doesn’t work even the printer head is opened and closed, but it can work when power on the printer._** 

**_* Please refer to printer model list for checking series printers._** 

###### **Example** 

The example below is operated in DOS environment via the parallel port connection to setup the label size, gap distance and sensor sensitivity. 

###### **C:\>COPY CON LPT1<ENTER>** 

**SIZE 4,2.5<ENTER>** 

**GAP 0.12,0<ENTER>** 

**SET GAP 1<ENTER>** 

**<CTRL><Z><ENTER>** 

###### **C:\>** 

###### **_Note:_** 

**_<ENTER> stands for keyboard "ENTER" key. In the above example, please press "ENTER" key instead of typing <ENTER> in the above example. <CTRL> stands for keyboard "Ctrl" key._** 

###### **<u>Troubleshooting:</u>** 

Press the FEED key to test. Does printer stop at the same position on each label without the error light blinking? If not, adjust the setting to a larger number. When adjusting this setting, begin from 0 and then on to higher values ~~i~~ ncrementally. 

###### **See Also** 

SIZE, GAP, BLINE 

**<mark>287</mark>** 

**SET BLINE** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is using to reverse/obverse the sensor function. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET BLINE REVERSE/OBVERSE** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|REVERSE|Reverse the sensor function. Redefine the reflective area is black line and<br>non-reflective part is paper. (Normally, reflective part is paper and non-<br>reflective part is black line.)|
|OBVERSE|Disable the "SET BLINE REVERSE" function.|



**<mark>288</mark>** 

###### **SET BLINE PRINTSIDE & SET BLINE BACKSIDE** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is using to set the top or bottom black mark sensor as the main transmitter. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET BLINE PRINTSIDE** 

###### **SET BLINE BACKSIDE** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**||
|---|---|---|
|PRINTSIDE|The light of the sensor will face toward the pri|nt side of the label.|
|BACKSIDE|The light of the sensor will face toward the ba|ck side of the label.|
||Supported printer model|Default|
||TDM-30|Print side|
||Alpha-30L|Back side|
||Alpha-40L|Back side|
||MH241 Series|Back side|
||MX241P Series|Back side|
||PEX-1001 Series|Back side|



**<mark>289</mark>** 

**SET HEAD** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is used to enable/disable head open sensor. If the head open sensor is turned off, an open printer head will not return an error message. This setting will be saved in printer memory. This command is only available for TSPL2 printers. 

Note: Please refer to printer model list for checking TSPL2 printers. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET HEAD ON /OFF** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Turn on the "HEAD OPEN" sensor|
|OFF|Turn off the "HEAD OPEN" sensor|



###### **Example** 

SET HEAD ON 

SET HEAD OFF 

**<mark>290</mark>** 

**SET RIBBON** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is used to enable/disable ribbon sensor detection. (Thermal Transfer Printing/Thermal Direct Printing) Printer will detect the presence of a ribbon to determine using either direct thermal or thermal transfer printing upon printer startup. This setting will NOT be saved in printer memory. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET RIBBON ON/OFF/INSIDE/OUTSIDE** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Thermal transfer printing|
|OFF|Thermal direct printing|
|INSIDE|The ribbon is inside wound. For TTP-384M only.**_*Since V6.80EZ._**|
|OUTSIDE|The ribbon is outside wound. For TTP-384M only. **_*Since V6.80EZ._**|



###### **Example** 

|**Sample Code**|
|---|
|**REM *****Disable ribbon detection sensor for direct thermal printing.**<br>**SET RIBBON OFF**|
|**SIZE 4,1**|
|**GAP 0,0**|
|**CLS**|
|**TEXT 10,10, " 3 "   ,0,1,1, " Direct thermal printing. "**<br>**PRINT 1**|
|**REM *****Enable ribbon detection sensor for thermal transfer printing.**<br>**SET RIBBON ON**|
|**SIZE 4,1**|
|**GAP 0,0**|
|**CLS**|
|**TEXT 10,10, " 3 "   ,0,1,1, " Thermal transfer printing. "**|
|**PRINT 1**|
|**REM *****For using ink-in ribbon in TTP-384M.**|



**<mark>291</mark>** 

###### **SET RIBBON INSIDE** 

**SIZE 4,1 GAP 0,0** 

###### **CLS** 

**TEXT 10,10, " 3 "  ,0,1,1, "  TTP-384M is using ink-in ribbon.** " 

**PRINT 1** 

**REM *****For using ink-out ribbon in TTP-384M.** 

**SET RIBBON OUTSIDE SIZE 4,1 GAP 0,0** 

**CLS** 

**TEXT 10,10, " 3 "   ,0,1,1, " TTP-384M is using ink-out ribbon. "** 

**PRINT 1** 

**<mark>292</mark>** 

**SET ENCODER** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is used to enable/disable ribbon encoder sensor detection. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET ENCODER ON/OFF** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Enable ribbon encoder sensor.|
|OFF|Disable ribbon encoder sensor.|



###### **Example** 

SET ENCODER ON 

SET ENCODER OFF 

**<mark>293</mark>** 

**SET RIBBONEND** 

###### **Description** 

This setting is used to enable/disable ribbon-end sensor detection. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET RIBBONEND ON/OFF** 

|**Parameter**<br>**Description**|
|---|
|ON<br>Enable ribbon-end sensor.|
|OFF<br>Disable ribbon-end sensor.|
|**_Note:_**|
|**_This command has been supported since V6.91 EZ and later firmware._**|



###### **Example** 

SET RIBBONEND ON 

SET RIBBONEND OFF 

**<mark>294</mark>** 

**SET COM1** 

###### **Description** 

This setting defines communication parameters for printer serial port. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET COM1 baud,parity,data,stop** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|baud|Baud rate, available baud rates are as listed :|
||24: 2400 bps|
||48: 4800 bps|
||96: 9600 bps|
||19: 19200 bps|
||38: 38400 bps|
||57: 57600 bps|
||115: 115200 bps|
|parity|Parity check|
||N: No parity check|
||E: Even parity check|
||O: Odd parity check|
|Data|Data bit|
||8: 8 bits data|
||7: 7 bits data|
|stop|Stop bit|
||1: 1 stop bit|
||2: 2 stop bits|



###### **Example** 

The parallel port is used to setup the printer serial port in this example via MS-DOS mode. 

**C:\>COPY CON LPT1<ENTER>** 

**SET COM1 19,N,8,1<ENTER>** 

**_<CTRL><Z><ENTER>_** 

**<mark>295</mark>** 

###### **C:\>** 

###### **_Note:_** 

**_<ENTER> stands for PC keyboard "ENTER" key. <CTRL><Z> means to hold PC keyboard "CTRL" key then press the PC keyboard <Z> key._** 

**<mark>296</mark>** 

**SET PRINTKEY** 

###### **Description** 

This command will print one label and feed label gap to tear bar position for tearing away. Press FEED button to print the next label or batch of labels. If label content includes serial text or barcode, it will change the serial number accordingly. This setting will be saved in printer memory. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET PRINTKEY OFF/ON/AUTO/<num>** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|Disable this function|
|ON|Enable this function|
|AUTO|Enable this function|
|<num>|Numbers of labels will be printed if FEED button is pressed.|



**Note: This command is only available for TSPL2 printers. Please refer to printer model list for checking TSPL2 printers.** 

###### **Example** 

###### **Sample code** 

**SIZE 4,2.5 GAP 0.12,0 SET PRINTKEY ON SET COUNTER @0 1 @0= "0001" CLS TEXT 10,10, "5",0,1,1,@0 PRINT 1** 

###### **Execute:** 

|**Syntax**|**Receive "PRINT m"**|**Print Out**|
|---|---|---|
|**SET PRINTKEY ON** or|1.) PRINT 2|Label 1~2|
|**SET PRINTKEY AUTO**|2.) Press FEED key|Label 3~4|



**<mark>297</mark>** 

|**Syntax**|**Receive "PRINT m,n"**|**Print Out**|
|---|---|---|
|**SET PRINTKEY ON**or|1.) PRINT 1,2|Label 1, Label 1|
|**SET PRINTKEY AUTO**|2.) Press FEED key|Label 2, Label 2|
|**Syntax**|**Receive "PRINT -1,n"**|**Print Out**|
|**SET PRINTKEY ON**or|1.) PRINT -1,2|Label 1, Label 1|
|**SET PRINTKEY AUTO**|2.) Press FEED key|Label 1, Label 1|
|**Syntax**|**Receive "PRINT m"**|**Print Out**|
|**SET PRINTKEY 5**|1.) PRINT 2|Label 1~2|
||2.) Press FEED key|Label 3~7|
|**Syntax**|**Receive "PRINT m,n"**|**Print Out**|
|**SET PRINTKEY 5**|1.) PRINT 1,2|Label 1, Label 1|
||2.) Press FEED key|Label 2~6|
|**Syntax**|**Receive "PRINT –1,n"**|**Print Out**|
|**SET PRINTKEY 5**|1.) PRINT -1,2|Label 1, Label 1|
||2.) Press FEED key|Label 1, Label 1|



**<mark>298</mark>** 

**SET REPRINT** 

###### **Description** 

This command will disable/enable a reprinting attempt subsequent to a "no paper", "no ribbon" or "carriage open" error. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET REPRINT OFF/ON** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|Disable this function|
|ON|Enable this function|
|**Note: This command**<br>**printers.**|**is only available for TSPL2 printers. Please refer toprinter model listfor checking TSPL2**|



###### **Example** 

SET REPRINT ON 

**<mark>299</mark>** 

**SET FEED_LEN** 

###### **Description** 

This command can set the feeding length when FEED key is pressed. This setting will be memorized by printer. The initialized value is the label length. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET FEED_LEN n** 

|**Parameter**<br>**Description**|
|---|
|n<br>The feeding length in dot.|
|**_Note:_**|
|**_This command has been supported since V5.10 EZ and later firmware._**|



###### **Example** 

|**Sample code**|**Result**|
|---|---|
|**SET FEED_LEN 100**|The feeding length is 100 dots when you press the FEED button<br>after this setting.|



**<mark>300</mark>** 

**GETSENSOR()** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to get the sensor status/AD value (Analogue-to-Digital value). We can use it to check the sensor function. 

###### **Syntax** 

**GETSENSOR(sensor$[,intension])** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|||
|---|---|---|---|
|sensor$|Sensor type.|||
||**GAP**|Gap senso|r|
||**BLINE**|Black mar|k sensor|
||**RIBBON**|Ribbon-en|d sensor|
||**PEEL**|Peeler se|nsor|
||**HEAD UP**|Thermal p|rint head open sensor|
||**HEAD TEMP**|The temp|erature of print head|
||**HEAD VOLT**|The volta|ge of print head|
||**BATTERY VOLT**|The volta|ge of battery (since A2.05 EZC)|
||**BATTERY CAP**|The capac|ity of battery (since A2.05 EZC)|
|intension|Sensor intension.|||
||**Gap**intension||Please refer to SET GAP for gap sensor range<br>of different model.|
||**BLINE**intension||Please refer to SET GAP for black mark<br>sensor range of different model.|
||**RIBBON**intensio|n|0 ~ 3|
||**PEEL**sensor inte|nsion|Ignored|
||**HEAD UP**sensor|intension|Ignored|
||**HEAD TEMP**||Ignored|
||**HEAD VOLT**||Ignored|
|Returned value|**Gap**|Return th|e AD value of gap sensor|
||**BLINE**|Return th|e AD value of black mark sensor|
||**RIBBON**|Return th|e AD value of ribbon sensor|
||**PEEL**|The retur<br>0: Paper i|n value will be either 0 or 1<br>s not on the sensor|



**<mark>301</mark>** 

|||1: Paper is on the sensor|
|---|---|---|
||**HEAD UP**|The return value will be either 0 or 1<br>0: print head module is close<br>1: print head module is open|
||**HEAD TEMP**|Return the temperature of thermal print head|
||**HEAD VOLT**|Return the voltage of thermal print head|
|**_Note:_**|||
|**_This command ha_**|**_s been supported since V6._**|**_75 EZ and later firmware._**|

**GETSETTING$()** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to get printer settings. 

###### **Syntax** 

**GETSETTING$ (app$,sec$,key$[,default$])** 

|**Parameter**<br> <br>||**Description**|
|---|---|---|
|**app$**<br>**sec$**|**key$**||
||DPI|Return printer resolution|
||MODEL|Return printer model name|
||SERIAL|Return Printer serial number|
||VERSION|Return Printer firmware version|
||CHECKSUM|Return Printer firmware checksum|
|INFORMATION|PRINTQUALITY|Return Printer print mode (DRAFT,<br>STANDARD or OPTIMUM; see SET<br>PRINTQUALITY)<br>For Alpha-2R<br>series printer|
||STANDBYTIME|<br>Return Printer standby time (OFF or<br>number)|
||IMAGE LENGTH|Return image length value by dots. (Since A2.17)|
||IMAGE WIDTH|Return image width value by dots. (Since A2.17)|
||MILAGE|Return printed mileage (in dots)|
|SYSTEM<br>RECORD|LABEL COUNTER|Return the total number of prints|
||CUT COUNTER|Return cutter cuts|
|WLAN|RSSI|Return WIFI RSSI|
|DOTSCAN|BADDOT|Since A213<br>Return bad dot|
|INFORMATION|PRINTER STATUS|.<br>Return printer status|
|TPHID|PRINT QUALITY|Return Print Quality|
|RTC|YEAR|Return Year|
|RTC|MONTH|Return Month|
|RTC|DATE|Since A2.13<br>Return Date|
|RTC|HOUR|<br>Return Hour|
|RTC|MINUTE|Return Minute|
|RTC|SECOND|Return Second|
||CAPACITY|Return the total capacity of DRAM|
|FILE<br>DRAM|AVAILABLE|Return the available capacity of DRAM|
||PHYICAL|Return the Dram Phy space|



**<mark>305</mark>** 

|CAPACITY|Return the total capacity of FLASH||
|---|---|---|
|FLASH<br>AVAILABLE|Return the available capacity of FLASH||
|PHYICAL|Return the Flash Phy space||
|CAPACITY|Return the total capacity of CARD||
|CARD<br>AVAILABLE|Return the available capacity of CARD||
|<br>INSTALLED|Return the status of card. 1: installed; 0: no|ne installed.|
|PHYICAL|Return the Card Phy space||
|USB<br>PHYICAL|Return the USB Phy space||
|MAC ADDRESS|Return MAC address||
|IP ADDRESS|Return IP address||
|SUBNET MASK|Return Subnet Mask||
|<br>DEFAULT GATEWAY|Return default gateway||
|NET<br>RAW PORT|Return raw port||
|NAME|Return printer name||
|PRIMARY DNS|Return primary DNS|since V8.12 &|
|SECONDARY DNS|Return secondary DNS|A2.09|
|CONFIG<br>MAC ADDRESS|Return MAC address||
|IP ADDRESS|Return IP address||
|SUBNET MASK|Return Subnet Mask||
|DEFAULT GATEWAY|Return default gateway||
|WLAN<br>RAW PORT|Return raw port||
|SSID|Return SSID||
|REGION|Return WiFi region|Since A213|
|PRINT SERVER NAME|Return print server name|.|
|FREQUENCY|Return WiFi frequence||
|PIN CODE|Return BT Pin Code||
|NAME|Return BT Name||
|BT<br>MODE|Return BT Mode|Since A213|
|<br>SW VERSION|Return BT Version|.|
|MAC ADDRESS|Return BT Local MAC||
|PAIR MODE|Return BT Pair Mode||
|CONFIG<br>BAUD RATE|Return baud rate of COM port||
|<br>COM1<br>DATA BIT|Return data bit of COM port||
|<br>PARITY|Return parity of COM port||
|STOP BIT|Return stop bit of COM port||
|SENSOR TYPE|Return the current sensor type||
|SENSOR<br>CARRIAGE|Return the status of head open sensor.||
|<br>GAP INTENSION|Return intension of gap sensor.||
|BLINE INTENSION|Return intension of black mark sensor.||



**<mark>306</mark>** 

|CONTINUOUS INTENSION|Return intension of continuous sensor.|
|---|---|
|THRESHOLD|Return threshold|
|AUTO THRESHOLD|Since A2.13<br>Return auto Threshold|
|LABEL CAPACITY|Return label capacity|
|RIBBON CAPACITY|Return ribbon capacity|
|PRINT MODE|Return pos-print action.|
|DENSITY|Return print density|
|PAPER SIZE|Return paper size|
|GAP SIZE|Return gap size|
|BLINE SIZE|Return black mark size|
|DIRECTION|Return printing direction|
|MIRROR|Return mirror status|
|RIBBON|Return ribbon status|
|REPRINT|Return reprint status|
|PAPER WIDTH|Return paper width|
|LIMIT FEED|Return maximum length for sensor calibration.|
|TSPL<br>OFFSET|Return OFFSET value.|
|REFERENCE X|Return REFERENCE X value|
|REFERENCE Y|Return REFERENCE Y value|
|SHIFT X|Return SHIFT X value|
|SHIFT Y|Return SHIFT Y value|
|SPEED|Return print speed|
|COUNTRY CODE|Return COUNTRY code|
|CODEPAGE|Return CODEPAGE|
|GAP OFFSET|Return gap offset value|
|RIBBON SENSOR|Since A2.13<br>Return ribbon sensor value|
|RIBBON ENCODER|<br>Return ribbon encoder value|
|CUT PIECE|Return cutter piece|
|**Parameter**<br>**Description**||
|[default$]<br>Optional. Expression conta<br>setting. If omitted, default|ining the value to return if no value is set in the key$ is assumed to be a zero-length string ("").|
|**_Note: This command has been supported since V6.72 EZ a_**|**_nd later firmware._**|

**SET USBHOST** 

###### **Description** 

This command can set the USB host for the usage of USB keyboard or scanner. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET USBHOST KEYBOARD/SCANNER** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|KEYBOARD|USB keyboard (Enable the prompt shown on LCD)|
|SCANNER|USB scanner (Disable the prompt shown on LCD)|
|**_Note:_**||
| **_This command ha_**<br> **_This command is_**|**_s been supported since V6.95 EZ and later firmware._**<br>**_for the model which has USB HOST connector._**|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code SET USBHOST KEYBOARD DOWNLOAD "A.BAS" :LOOP SIZE 4,2 GAP 0,0 CLS INPUT A$ TEXT 50,50, "0",0,20,20,A$ PRINT 1 GOTO LOOP EOP A.BAS** 

**<mark>310</mark>** 

**SET AUTORUN** 

###### **Description** 

This command redefines the BAS file which can be run automatically while switching on the printer. Default is AUTO.BAS. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET AUTORUN "filename"** 

**<u>Parameter Description</u>** filename The file will be defined to AUTO-RUN file. Default is AUTO.BAS. **_Note: This command has been supported since V6.86 EZ and later firmware._** 

###### **Example** 

**Sample Code REM *****Step1: Send the following command to redefine the auto-run file from "AUTO.BAS" to "TEST.BAS"** 

**SET AUTORUN "TEST.BAS"** 

**REM *****Step2: Send the following commands to download "TEST.BAS" file into printer. DOWNLOAD F, "TEST.BAS" SIZE 4,1** 

**GAP 0,0** 

**DIRECTION 1** 

**CLS** 

**BLOCK 10,10,600,200, "3",0,1,1,12, "TEST.BAS is running automatically while turning on the printer. "** 

**PRINT 1 EOP** 

**REM *****Step3: Turn off and on the printer to run "TEST.BAS" automatically.** 

**Result** 

**<mark>311</mark>** 

“TEST .BAS” is running automatically while turning on the printer. 

<mark>|</mark> 

**SET RESPONSE** 

###### **Description** 

This command can response issue automatically. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET RESPONSE ["Job ID",] ON/OFF/BATCH** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|["Job ID"]|Optional. Set job ID. Default is Null|
|ON|Enable this function|
|OFF|Disable this function. Default is OFF|
|BATCH|Response at the end of printing job|
|**_Note:_**||
|**_This command has bee_**|**_n supported since V7.09 EZ and later firmware. _**|



###### **Response Syntax** 

**{Status,######,ID}** 

|**Status**||
|---|---|
|[Hex Rec|eive]|
||00 Normal|
||01 Head opened|
||02 Paper Jam|
||03 Paper Jam and head opened|
||04 Out of paper|
||05 Out of paper and head opened|
||08 Out of ribbon|
||09 Out of ribbon and head opened|
||0A Out of ribbon and paper jam|
||0B Out of ribbon, paper jam and head opened<br>0C Out of ribbon and out of paper|



**<mark>313</mark>** 

0D Out of ribbon, out of paper and head opened 10 Pause 20 Printing 80 Other error #####:  00001 ~ 99999 

**<mark>314</mark>** 

###### **Example** 

**Sample Code** 

**SET RESPONSE ON** 

**SIZE 4,2 GAP 0,0 PRINT 3** 

**{00,00001}{00,00002}{00,00003}** 

**SET RESPONSE "ID1",ON** 

**SIZE 4,2 GAP 0,0 PRINT 3,2** 

**{00,00001,ID1}{00,00002,ID1}{00,00003,ID1}{00,00004,ID1}{00,00005,ID1}{00,00006,ID1}** 

**SET RESPONSE "CCCC",BATCH SIZE 4,2 GAP 0,0 PRINT 3,2** 

**{00,00006,CCCC}** 

**<mark>315</mark>** 

**SET DAYLIGHT_SAVE** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set daylight saving time. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET DAYLIGHT_SAVE ON/OFF** 

**SET DAYLIGHT_SAVE "Start",** " **End"** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Enable function|
|OFF|Disable function (Default)|
|"Start"|The time will be increased 1 hour from "Start time"|
|"End"|The time will be reduced 1 hour (return) from "End time"|
||Month<br>"JAN","FEB","MAR","APR","MAY","JUN","JUL","AUG","SEP","OCT","NOV","DEC"<br>"JANUARY", "FEBRUARY", "MARCH", "APRIL", "MAY", "JUNE", "JULY", "AUGUST", "SEPTEMBER",<br>"OCTOBER", "NOVEMBER", "DECEMBER"|
||Week<br>"SUN","MON","TUE","WED","THU","FRI","SAT"<br>"SUNDAY", "MONDAY", "TUESDAY", "WEDNESDAY", "THURSDAY", "FRIDAY", "SATURDAY"|
||Which Week<br>"FIRST","SECOND", "THIRD","FOURTH","LAST"<br>"1<sup>ST</sup>", "2<sup>ND</sup>", "3<sup>RD</sup>", "4<sup>TH</sup>", "LAST"|
||Date<br>1~31|
||Time<br>0:00~23:00|
|**_Note:_**||
|**_This comman_**|**_d has been supported since V8.03 EZ and later firmware._**|



**<mark>316</mark>** 

###### **Example** 

**Sample Code SET DAYLIGHT_SAVE ON SET DAYLIGHT_SAVE OFF SET DAYLIGHT_SAVE "MAR 1 4:00","NOV 1 5:00" SET DAYLIGHT_SAVE "MAR FIRST SUN 2:00", "NOV LAST SUN 3:00"** 

**<mark>317</mark>** 

###### **SET REGISTRATION** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set the mode of label positioning for the label roll with multiple sizes labels. 

###### **Syntax** 

###### **SET REGISTRATION mode** 

**<u>Parameter Description</u>** mode BYSIZE: Original registration (Default) ACTUAL: For handle label rolls that have different label sizes on the same roll. **_Note: This command has been supported since A2.12 EZD and later firmware._ Sample Code Result** 150 <u>jobs on 2 labels:</u> **SET REGISTRATION ACTUAL SIZE 4,1 GAP 0.12,0 CLS TEXT 40,40,"0",0,10,10,"1**<sup>**st**</sup> **Label 4x1" PRINT 1 SIZE 4,5 GAP 0.12,0 CLS TEXT 40,40,"0",0,10,10,"2**<sup>**nd**</sup> **Label 4x5" PRINT 1** 

###### **Example** 

**<mark>318</mark>** 

150 <u>job with 2 labels:</u> 

###### **SET REGISTRATION ACTUAL** 

**SIZE 4,6.12** 

**GAP 0.12,0** 

**CLS** 

**TEXT 40,40,"0",0,10,10,"1**<sup>**st**</sup> **Label 4x1"** 

**TEXT 40,267,"0",0,10,10,"2**<sup>**nd**</sup> **Label 4x5"** 

**PRINT 1** 



**<mark>319</mark>** 

**SET APPLICATOR** 

###### **Description** 

Used for print & apply application, the label is moved forward to be removed by applicator, and applied to an item. Only printer with GPIO interface supported this mode. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET APPLICATOR ON/OFF** 

|**Return Value**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Enable the applicator function|
|OFF|Disable the applicator function|
|**_Note:_**||
|**_This command has bee_**|**_n supported since A2.15 EZD and later firmware._**|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code** 

**SET APPLICATOR ON** 

**<mark>320</mark>** 

**SET MENULOCK** 

###### **Description** 

This command can enable or disable menulock funciton, or setup password of it. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET MENULOCK n** 

|**Return Value**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Enable the menulock function|
|OFF|Disable the menulock function|
|n|0000 <= "n" <= 9999, 4 digits number to setup password|
|**_Note:_**||
|**_This command has be_**|**_en supported since A2.17 EZD and later firmware._**|



###### **Example** 

###### **Sample code** 

**REM *****Enable funciton***** SET MENULOCK ON REM *****Setup password as 1234******* 

**SET MENULOCK "1234"** 

**<mark>321</mark>** 

**PEEL** 

###### **Description** 

This command obtains the status of the peel-off sensor. This attribute is read only. 

###### **Syntax** 

**PEEL** 

|**Return Value**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|0|Paper is not on top of peel sensor|
|1|Paper is on top of peel sensor|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code DOWNLOAD "DEMO.BAS" SIZE 4,1 GAP 0,0 SET PEEL OFF SET KEY1 OFF SET LED1 OFF SET LED3 OFF :START LED1=0 LED3=0 IF KEY1=1 THEN GOTO A GOTO START :A LED1=1 CLS TEXT 10,10, "3",0,1,1, "PEEL Function Test!! " PRINT 1,1 :B LED1=0 IF PEEL=1 THEN LED3=1** 

**<mark>322</mark>** 

###### **GOTO B** 

**ELSE CLS** 

**TEXT 10,10, "3",0,1,1, "The label is removed from the PEEL sensor!! " PRINT 1,1 GOTO START ENDIF EOP DEMO** 

**<mark>323</mark>** 

**LED1, LED2, LED3** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to control LED on/off. This attribute is write-only. Specify 1 to light on LED and 0 to turn off LED. Before using this command, be sure to cancel the default LED functions. Please refer to the SET LED command. 

###### **Syntax** 

**LED** **_m_ =** **_n_** 

|**Return Value**<br>**D**<br>m<br>m<br>m<br>m<br>n<br>0:<br>1:<br>|**escriptio**<br>=1, LED1<br>=2, LED2<br>=3, LED3<br>turn off<br>light on|**n**<br> <br> <br> <br>LED<br>LED||||||||
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|**Model**|**LED1**|**LED2**|**LED3**|**LED4**|**LED5**|**LED6**|**LED7**|**LED8**|**LED2 & LED3**|
|TDP-643 Plus/ 643R Plus series|ONLINE|ERROR|ERROR|||||||

**<mark>325</mark>** 

**KEY1, KEY2, KEY3** 

###### **Description** 

This command reads the status of KEY1, KEY2 and KEY3. 

|**Model**|**KEY0**|**KEY1**|**KEY2**|**KEY3**|**KEY4**|**KEY5**|**KEY6**|
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|TDP-643 Plus/643R Plus||PAUSE||||||
|TTP-243/243 Plus/243 Pro<br>series, TTP-244ME/244 ME<br>Plus/244M Pro series, TTP-244/<br>244 Plus series||PAUSE|FEED|||||
|TDP-245/247 series, TTP-<br>245/247 series, TTP-245C<br>series, TDP-225 series, TTP-225<br>series, TA200 series, Alpha-3R,<br>DA series, TE series, Alpha-2R,<br>TDM-20,TDM-30||FEED||||||
|TX200 series (with LCD), TC210<br>series (with LCD), TX600 series,<br>MX240P series,MH series||FEED|MENU|UP|RIGHT|LEFT|DOWN|
|TTP-246M series||MENU|PAUSE|FEED|(UP)|(DOWN)|(SELECT)|
|TTP-248M series||MENU|PAUSE|FEED||||
|TTP-2410M/2410M Pro series,<br>TTP-246M Plus/246M Pro<br>series, TTP-268M series, TTP-<br>384M series, ME240(LCD<br>controlpanel)series||MENU|PAUSE|FEED|UP|DOWN|SELECT|
|ME240 (Non-LCD control panel)<br>series||FEED|PAUSE|||||
|M23 series|FEED|LEFT|MID|RIGHT||||
|Alpha-4L||FEED|INFO|MENU||||
|MX240 series, TTP-2410MT/MU<br>series||PAUSE|MENU|FEED|UP|SELECT|DOWN|
|DH/TH series<br>**Note: Please refer toprinter m**|**odel list** **f**|FEED<br>**or checking**|PAUSE<br>**series print**|REPRINT<br>**ers.**||||



**Note: Please refer to printer model list for checking series printers.** 

###### **Syntax** 

**KEY** **_m_ =** **_n_** 

|**Key**|**Return Value**|
|---|---|
|KEY1 (MENU)|0: released<br>1: pressed|
|KEY2 (PAUDE)|0: released<br>1: pressed|
|KEY3 (FEED)|0: released<br>1: pressed|

**Read attribute: A=LABEL or A$=STR$(LABEL)** 

**<u>Parameter Description</u>** n Number of labels printed. 0<=n<=999999999

**<mark>337</mark>** 

**MONTH** 

###### **Description** 

This variable reads/writes the month data via the Real Time Clock (RTC). Two-digit (01~12) month formats are supported by RTC. 

###### **Syntax** 

**Write attribute: MONTH = 01** 

**Read attribute: A = MONTH** 

Range: 01~12 

###### **Example** 

|**Sample code**||
|---|---|
|**DOWNLOAD "SetMonth.BAS"**<br>**REM *****Set Month Parameter to RTC*******<br>**MONTH=01**<br>**EOP**||
|**SetMonth**||
|**Sample code**|**Result**|
|**DOWNLOAD "DEMO.BAS"**||
|**SIZE 4,1**<br>**GAP 0,0**||
|**DIRECTION 1**<br>**CLS**||
|**REM *****Read Month parameter form RTC*******<br>**MONTH$=STR$(MONTH)**<br>**M=MONTH**||
|**REM *****Print*******<br>**TEXT 10,10, "3",0,1,1, "MONTH1="+MONTH$**||
|**TEXT 10,50, "3",0,1,1, "MONTH2="+STR$(M)**||
|**TEXT 10,90, "3",0,1,1, "MONTH3="+STR$(MONTH)**||





**<mark>338</mark>** 

**PRINT 1 EOP DEMO**

~!C, MONTH, DATE, DAY, HOUR, MINUTE, SECOND

**<mark>344</mark>** 

**MINUTE** 

###### **Description** 

This variable reads/writes the minute data via the Real Time Clock (RTC).  Two-digits (00~59) minute format is supported by RTC. 

###### **Syntax** 

**Write attribute: MINUTE = 12 Read attribute: A = MINUTE** 

Range: 00~59 

###### **Example** 

**Sample code DOWNLOAD "SetMinute.BAS" REM *****Set Minute Parameter to RTC***** MINUTE=27 EOP SetMinute** 

|**Sample code**|**Result**|
|---|---|
|**DOWNLOAD "DEMO.BAS"**||
|**SIZE 4,1**||
|**GAP 0,0**||
|**DIRECTION 1**||
|**CLS**||
|**REM *****Read Minute parameter form RTC*******||
|**MINUTE$=STR$(MINUTE)**||
|**MIN=MINUTE**||
|**REM *****Print*******||
|**TEXT 10,10, "3",0,1,1, "MINUTE1="+MINUTE$**||
|**TEXT 10,50, "3",0,1,1, "MINUTE2="+STR$(MIN)**||





**<mark>345</mark>** 

**TEXT 10,90, "3",0,1,1, "MINUTE3="+STR$(MINUTE) PRINT 1 EOP DEMO** 

###### **See Also** 

~!C, MONTH, DATE, DAY, HOUR, MINUTE, SECOND 

**<mark>346</mark>** 

**SECOND** 

###### **Description** 

This variable reads/writes the second data via the Real Time Clock (RTC). Two-digits (00~59) second format is supported by RTC. 

###### **Syntax** 

**Write attribute: SECOND = 12** 

**Read attribute: A = SECOND** 

Range: 00~59 

###### **Example** 

|**Sample code**||
|---|---|
|**DOWNLOAD "SetSecond.BAS"**<br>**REM *****Set Second Parameter to RTC*******<br>**SECOND=59**<br>**EOP**||
|**SetSecond**||
|**Sample code**|**Result**|
|**DOWNLOAD "DEMO.BAS"**||
|**SIZE 4,1**<br>**GAP 0,0**<br>**DIRECTION 1**<br>**CLS**||
|**REM *****Read Second parameter form RTC*******<br>**SECOND$=STR$(SECOND)**<br>**SEC=SECOND**||
|**REM *****Print*******<br>**TEXT 10,10, "3",0,1,1, "SECOND1="+SECOND$**||
|**TEXT 10,50, "3",0,1,1, "SECOND2="+STR$(SEC)**||
|**TEXT 10,90, "3",0,1,1, "SECOND3="+STR$(SECOND)**||





**<mark>347</mark>** 

**PRINT 1 EOP DEMO**

**BT PAIRMODE** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set Bluetooth pair mode. (since A2.12) 

###### **Note: This function supports Mfi BM78 Bluetooth module only.** 

###### **Syntax** 

**BT PAIRMODE "mode"** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|mode|**LEGACY**: Legacy pairing mode (need to key-in pairing code)|
||**SSP_JUSTWORK**: Just work pairing mode (default)|
||**SSP_USERCONFIRM**: User configuration pairing mode (will ask if it be agreed to pair)|



###### **Example** 

|**Sample code**<br>**BT PAIRMODE LEGACY**|
|---|
|**BT PAIRMODE SSP_USERCONFIRM**|
|**BT PAIRMODE SSP_JUSTWORK**|



**<mark>361</mark>** 

###### **BT MODE** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set Bluetooth mode. 

###### **Note: This function supports Mfi Bluetooth module and Wi-Fi module.** 

###### **Syntax** 

**BT MODE mode (or "mode")** 

|**Parameter**<br>**Description**||
|---|---|
|mode (for Mfi Bluetooth module)<br>**Classic**|BT3.0 (Default)|
|**BLE**|BT4.0|
|**Dual**|DUAL|
|"mode" (for Wi-Fi module)<br>**Classic**|"BT2.1" (Default)|
|**BLE**|"BT4.0"|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code** (For Mfi Bluetooth module) **BT MODE BT4.0** <Reboot printer> **Sample code** (For Mfi Bluetooth module) **BT MODE DUAL** 

<Reboot printer>

**WLAN WPA** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set WPA security mode. This command only can be set but not be checked. Restart the printer is necessary. 

###### **Syntax** 

**WLAN WPA OFF** 

**WLAN WPA "key"** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|Disable WPA security mode.|
|Key|The network security key. 8 to 63 characters.<br>Key = Passphrase or Pre-Shared Key<br>(Passphrase is a string containing between 8 and 63 characters)<br>(Pre-Shared Key is a 32-byte key, formatted as hexadecimal number)|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code WLAN WPA OFF WLAN WPA "123456789"** 

**<mark>365</mark>** 

**WLAN WEP** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set WEP security mode. This command only can be set but not be checked. Restart the printer is necessary. 

###### **Syntax** 

**WLAN WEP OFF** 

**WLAN WEP n, "key"** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|Disable WEP security mode.|
|N|The index of key. 1 to 4.|
|Key|The encryption key. 5 or 13 characters or 10 or 26 hexadecimal digits.|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code WLAN WEP OFF WLAN WEP 1, "ABCDE" WLAN WEP 2, "ABCDE" WLAN WEP 3, "ABCDE" WLAN WEP 4, "4142434445"**

**<mark>375</mark>** 

###### **UHF RFID Setting Commands** 

Incorporate UHF RFID commands into new or existing printer programs. 

###### **IMPORTANT:** 

**With all examples make sure the label length matches the physical length of the installed media.** 

###### **RFID ON/OFF** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to enable/disable the RFID encoder module. 

###### **Syntax** 

**RFID ON/OFF** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Enable the RFID encoder module|
|OFF|Disable the RFID encoder module|



###### **Example** 

**RFID ON** 

**<mark>376</mark>** 

**RFID ERROR** 

###### **Description** 

If an error persists after the specified number of labels are tried, perform this error handling action. 

###### **Syntax** 

###### **RFID ERROR OFF/STOP/OVERSTRIKE** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|No specific action is taken when a tag fails to be programmed.|
|STOP|Place printer in Pause mode. The label is discarded and reprinting of the label (if<br>desired) must be initiated from the host. When the error is cleared, the label with<br>the failed tag moves forward until the next label is in position to be printed.|
|OVERSTRIKE|Each failed label prints with the Overstrike pattern and the form retries on a new<br>label until the Label Retry count is exhausted.|



###### **Example** 

**RFID ERROR OVERSTRIKE** 

**<mark>377</mark>** 

**RFID RETRY** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set the number of label retries that the RFID encoder will attempt before declaring a fault. 

###### **Syntax** 

**RFID RETRY #** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|#|Number of retries (1 – 10)|



###### **Example** 

**RFID RETRY 2** 

**<mark>378</mark>** 

###### **RFID RETRYERROR ON/OFF** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set if errors are declared when the Label Retry count is exceeded. 

###### **Syntax** 

**RFID RETRYERROR ON/OFF** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Enable the RFID retry error function|
|OFF|Disable the RFID retry error function|



###### **Example** 

**RFID RETRYERROR OFF** 

**<mark>379</mark>** 

**RFID POSITION** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set the how far the RFID tag encoding position of the currently installed tag should be offset from Top of Form. Normally, this value is set automatically by the RFID calibration process and should not be changed. 

###### **Syntax** 

**RFID POSITION #** <u>English system (inch)</u> **RFID POSITION # mm** <u>Metric system (mm)</u> **RFID POSITION # dot** <u>Dot measurement</u> 

**<u>Parameter Description</u>** # Position of the antenna (inch/ mm/ dot) 



###### **Example** 

**RFID POSITION 60 dot** 

**<mark>380</mark>** 

**RFID POWER** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set the for optimal tag encoding. Sets the read/write power level to be used in the RFID encoder. Normally, this value is set automatically by the RFID calibration process and should not be changed. 

###### **Syntax** 

**RFID POWER read,write** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|||
|---|---|---|---|
|Read|Custom tag read pow|er level setting.||
|Write|Custom tag write po|wer level setting.||
||Accepted Values:|||
|||read|write|
||Alpha-40L|1 – 27|1 – 27|
||PEX-2000|1 – 30|1 – 30|
||TH240/TH340|1 – 27|1 – 27|
||MB241/MB341|1 – 27|1 – 27|



###### **Example** 

**RFID POWER 12,16** 

**<mark>381</mark>** 

**RFID COUNTRESET** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to clear the total/failed tag statistics counters. 

###### **Syntax** 

**RFID COUNTRESET** 

**<u>Parameter Description</u>** N/A 



###### **Example** 

**RFID COUNTRESET** 

**<mark>382</mark>** 

**RFID READ/WRITE** 

###### **Description** 

This command allows you to write or read to a UHF RFID tag. 

###### **Syntax** 

###### **RFID a,b,format,offset,size,memory bank,data** 

|**Parameter**<br>**Description**|
|---|
|a<br>**WRITE**= write to the tag<br>**READ**= read the tag<br> <br><br> <br>|
|b<br>**WRITE**<br>only<br>lock<br>password<br>0 = write without lock.<br>1 to FFFFFFFF in hex =write and lockthe data<br>block to prevent it from<br>being overwritten.|
|**READ**<br>only<br>unlock<br>password<br>0 = read without unlock.<br>1 to FFFFFFFF in hex =read and unlockthe data<br>block so it can be<br>overwritten later.|
|Format<br>A letter specifying the representation format of the field data.<br>**A**= ASCII<br>**H**= Hex|
|offset<br>This optional parameter of starting position to do the write/read relative to the<br>start of the mem bank. The position is a word value (16 bits).|
|Size<br>A decimal number specifying the overall bit length of the RFID tag memory bank<br>that will be read starting at the offset position (not necessarily the total bank size).<br>**Note:**<br>-<br>**When using WRITE, if the "size" is larger than the "data", it will be padded with 0 in front of**<br>**the data to read.**<br>-<br>**When using READ, if the "size" is larger than the WRITE "data", it will be padded with 0 in back**<br>**of the data to read.**<br>-<br>**Refer to the example 3 of sample code.**|
|memory bank<br>**EPC**<br>EPC 12 bytes data area|
|**TID**<br>Tag identification 8 bytes area (currently not applicable for<br>RFID WRITE)|
|**USR**<br>User 32 bytes area|
|**ACS**<br>4 bytes access code area|

**CLS RFID WRITE,12345678,H,0,12,EPC,"123456789012" RFID WRITE,12345678,H,0,12,USR,"987654321012" RFID WRITE,12345678,H,0,8,ACS,"12345678" RFID READ,12345678,H,0,8,ACS,"ACS : " RFID READ,12345678,H,0,12,EPC,"EPC : " RFID READ,12345678,H,0,12,USR,"USR : " PRINT 1** 

**Example 6 (EPC & USR & ACS & KIL with Lock)**

**<mark>388</mark>** 

**RFID RFLOCK** 

###### **Description** 

Perform different types of RFID lock operations on available RFID data fields. 

###### **Syntax** 

**RFID RFLOCK,Type[,Field][,BlockStart,BlockQuantity][,Format,Passcode]** 

|**Parameter**|**Descript**|**ion**|
|---|---|---|
|Type|LOCK, U|NLOCK, PERMALOCK, PERMAUNLOCK, PERMABLOCK, PERMACHIP|
|Field|EPC|Electronic Product Code memory bank|
||USR|User memory bank|
||ACS|Access Password|
||KIL|Kill Password|
|BlockStart|USR Bloc|k Permalock Start address|
|BlockQuantity|USR Bloc|k Permalock Quantity|
|Format|A, H||
|Passcode|The valu<br>bits.<br>**Note: Th**|e of the passcode for the lock operations. The size for the passcode is 32<br>**e passcode must be non-zero when "Type" is LOCK or UNLOCK.**|



###### **Example** 

**<mark>Sample code 1</mark>** Write the password as 12345678 to lock the EPC memory bank. **CLS RFID RFLOCK,LOCK,EPC,,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** **<mark>Sample code 2</mark>** Write the password as 12345678 to unlock the EPC memory bank. **CLS RFID RFLOCK,UNLOCK,EPC,,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

**<mark>389</mark>** 

**<mark>Sample code 3</mark>** Write the password as 12345678 to permanently lock the EPC memory bank. **CLS RFID RFLOCK,PERMALOCK,EPC,,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** **<mark>Sample code 4</mark>** Write the password as 12345678 to permanently unlock the EPC memory bank. **CLS RFID RFLOCK,PERMAUNLOCK,EPC,,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** **<mark>Sample code 5</mark>** Write the password as 12345678 to lock the USR memory bank. **CLS RFID RFLOCK,LOCK,,USR,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** **<mark>Sample code 6</mark>** Write the password as 12345678 to lock the ACS memory bank. **CLS RFID RFLOCK,LOCK,,,ACS,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** **<mark>Sample code 7</mark>** Write the password as 12345678 to lock the EPC and USR memory bank. **CLS RFID RFLOCK,LOCK,EPC,USR,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** **<mark>Sample code 8</mark>** Write the password as 12345678 to lock the EPC, USR, and ACS memory bank. **CLS RFID RFLOCK,LOCK,EPC,USR,ACS,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** **<mark>Sample code 9 (PERMACHIP)</mark> CLS RFID RFLOCK,PERMACHIP PRINT 1** 

**<mark>Sample code 10 (USR PERMABLOCK)</mark> CLS RFID RFLOCK,PERMABLOCK,1,2,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

**<mark>390</mark>** 

**CLS RFID RFLOCK,PERMABLOCK,0,3,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

###### **<mark>Sample code 11 (EPC)</mark>** 

**CLS RFID RFLOCK,LOCK,EPC,,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

**CLS** 

**RFID RFLOCK,UNLOCK,EPC,,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

**CLS RFID RFLOCK,PERMALOCK,EPC,,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

###### **CLS** 

**RFID RFLOCK,PERMAUNLOCK,EPC,,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

**<mark>Sample code 12 (USR)</mark>** 

**CLS** 

**RFID RFLOCK,LOCK,,USR,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

**CLS RFID RFLOCK,LOCK,EPC,USR,,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

**CLS RFID RFLOCK,LOCK,EPC,USR,ACS,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

**<mark>Sample code 13 (ACS)</mark>** 

**CLS RFID RFLOCK,LOCK,,,ACS,,H,12345678 PRINT 1** 

**<mark>391</mark>** 

**RFIDDETECT** 

###### **Description** 

This command will calibrate the RFID and determine the position of the RFID chip. (Since A2.16) 

###### **Syntax** 

**RFIDDETECT** 

**<u>Parameter Description</u>** N/A 



###### **Example** 

**RFIDDETECT** 

**<mark>392</mark>** 

###### **HF RFID Setting Commands** 

Incorporate HF RFID commands into new or existing printer programs. 

###### **IMPORTANT:** 

**With all examples make sure the label length matches the physical length of the installed media.** 

###### **RFID ON/OFF** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to enable/disable the RFID encoder module. 

###### **Syntax** 

**RFID ON/OFF** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|ON|Enable the RFID encoder module|
|OFF|Disable the RFID encoder module|



###### **Example** 

**RFID ON** 

**<mark>393</mark>** 

**RFID ERROR** 

###### **Description** 

If an error persists after the specified number of labels are tried, perform this error handling action. 

###### **Syntax** 

###### **RFID ERROR OFF/STOP/OVERSTRIKE** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|No specific action is taken when a tag fails to be programmed.|
|STOP|Place printer in Pause mode. The label is discarded and reprinting of the label (if<br>desired) must be initiated from the host. When the error is cleared, the label with<br>the failed tag moves forward until the next label is in position to be printed.|
|OVERSTRIKE|Each failed label prints with the Overstrike pattern and the form retries on a new<br>label until the Label Retry count is exhausted.|



###### **Example** 

**RFID ERROR OVERSTRIKE**

**RFID READ/WRITE** 

###### **Description** 

This command allows you to write or read to a HF RFID (13.56 MHz) tag. Please note that the RFID POSITION must be set before performing any read/write operations. 

###### **Syntax** 

**RFID a,b,c,d,format,offset,size,memory bank,data** 

|**Parameter**<br>**Descriptio**|**n**|
|---|---|
|a<br>**WRITE**= w<br>**READ**= rea|rite to the tag<br>d the tag|
|b<br>HF RFID ta<br>|g types (13.56 MHz)<br>|
|**15693**|Vicinity|
|**14443A**|Proximity|
|**FELICA**|Proximity|
|c<br>IC Chip, Set<br>|to 0 for now, reserved for future use.<br>|
|d<br>WRITE<br>(only)|**0**= write without lock.<br>**1**= write and lock|
|READ|no effect|
|Format<br>A letter sp<br>**A**= ASCII<br>**H**= Hex<br>|ecifying the representation format of the field data.|
|offset<br>This option<br>start of the<br>(UID alway|al parameter of starting position to do the write/read relative to the<br>mem bank. The position is a word value (16 bits).<br>s set 0)|
|Size<br>A decimal<br>that will be<br>|number specifying the overall bit length of the RFID tag memory bank<br>read starting at the offset position (UID always set 0).|
|memory bank<br>**UID**|Unique Identifier|
|**USR**|User Memory (Variable size)|
|**AFI**|1 byte AFI code(15693 only)|
|**DSFID**|1 byte DSFID code(15693 only)|
|**EAS**|1 bit EAS operation(15693 only)|
|data<br>**WRITE**= co<br>**READ**= [pr|ntent of data string<br>ompt of data]|
|**Note:**<br>-<br>**RFID WRI**<br>- **[  ] = Opt**|**TE supported "string" or basic variable (e.g. VAR$)**<br>**ional parameter**|

###### ~~<u>Tt</u>~~ 

**NFC TIMEOUT** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set the timeout for the current read/write operation (in seconds) 0 to 3600, setting to 0 disables the timeout feature. 

###### **Syntax** 

**NFC TIMEOUT m** 

**<u>Parameter Description</u>** m 0 to 3600 **_Note:_** ▪ **_The default value is 10 seconds when printer initializes._** ▪ **_The printer will beep for notice when it’s timeout._** 

###### **Example** 

**Sample code NFC TIMEOUT 20** 

**<mark>400</mark>** 

**NFC READ** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to return content stored in the last NFC read event. (Max. of 2048 characters) 

###### **Syntax** 

**NFC READ** 

**<u>Parameter Description</u>** None N/A 

###### **Example** 

**Sample code** 

**NFC READ** 

**<mark>401</mark>** 

**NFC WRITE** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set the content to be transmitted by the NFC system. (Max. of 2048 characters) 

###### **Syntax** 

**NFC WRITE "content"** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|content|Content of text string|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code NFC WRITE "Test"** 

**<mark>402</mark>** 

**NFC MODE** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set the NFC reader mode. This commend can start or stop a read or write operation. (Max. of 2048 characters) 

###### **Syntax** 

**NFC MODE OFF/READ/WRITE** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|Disable|
|READ|Read tag mode|
|WRITE|Write tag mode|



**_Note:_** 

**_The default value is "OFF". It returns to "OFF" after a read or write operation completes, fails or times out. For continue to write or read data to tag, set this value to the desired "READ" or "WRITE"._** 

###### **Example** 

|**Sample code**||
|---|---|
|**Write data to tag once**|**Continue to write data to tag**|
|**NFC MODE OFF**|**NFC MODE WRITE**|
|**NFC TIMEOUT 3**|**NFC WRITE "123456789"**|
|**NFC WRITE "Test"**|**Continue to read data from Tag**|
|**Read data from tag once**<br>**NFC MODE OFF**|**NFC MODE READ**<br>**NFC READ**|
|**NFC TIMEOUT 3**||
|**NFC READ**||



**<mark>403</mark>** 

###### **Alpha-2R/TDM Series Setting Commands** 

###### **SET PRINTQUALITY** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set the print mode (print quality) for Alpha-2R and TDM series printer. 

(Supported device: Alpha-2R and TDM series only) 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET PRINTQUALITY DRAFT/STANDARD/OPTIMUM** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|DRAFT|High print speed with lower density|
|STANDARD|Standard print speed and quality|
|OPTIMUM|According to the label content such as barcode, text, and graphic to lower the print<br>speed for getting higher print quality|
|**_Note:_**||
|**_The default value is_**|**_"STANDARD"._**|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code** 

**SET PRINTQUALITY DRAFT SET PRINTQUALITY STANDARD** 

**SET PRINTQUALITY OPTIMUM** 

**<mark>404</mark>** 

**SET STANDBYTIME** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set the standby time for Alpha-2R and TDM series printer. 

(Supported device: Alpha-2R and TDM series only) 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET STANDBYTIME OFF/XXXXX** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|Disable|
|XXXXX|0 ~ 65534 (second)|
|**_Note:_**||
|**_The default value is_**|**"SET STANDBYTIME 120"****_._**|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code SET STANDBYTIME OFF** 

**SET STANDBYTIME 480** 

**<mark>405</mark>** 

**SET SLEEPTIME** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to set the sleeping time for Alpha-2R and TDM series printer. 

(Supported device: Alpha-2R and TDM series only) 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET SLEEPTIME OFF/XXXXX** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|OFF|Disable|
|XXXXX|0 ~ 65534 (minute)|
|**_Note:_**||
|**_The default value is_**|**"SET SLEEPTIME 30"****_._**|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code** 

**SET SLEEPTIME OFF** 

**SET SLEEPTIME 20** 

**<mark>406</mark>** 

###### **GPIO Setting Commands** 

###### **SET GPO** 

###### **Description** 

Use this command to send out the GPIO signals by the printer. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET GPOn signal state, delay0, pulse0, delay1, pulse1, function condition** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|n|n = 1 ~ 7|
|Signal state|Seven dedicated outputs are available for the desired function conditions.<br>|
||**HIGH**<br>Goes the high level signal when the following function<br>condition is detected.|
||**LOW**<br>Goes the low level signal when the following function<br>condition is detected.|
||**POS**<br>Goes the positive pulse signal when the following function<br>condition is detected.|
||**NEG**<br>Goes the negative pulse signal when the following function<br>condition is detected.|
|Delay0|After detecting the following function condition, the printer will wait this period<br>of time before sending out the "true" output signal.<br>Unit: millisecond. Maximum: 32000.|
|Pulse0|Pulse width corresponding to the function condition becoming "true". (Ignored<br>for level-type signals.)<br>Unit: millisecond. Maximum: 32000.|
|Delay1|After detecting the following function condition, the printer will wait this period<br>of time before sending out the "false" output signal.<br>Unit: millisecond. Maximum: 32000.|
|Pulse1|Pulse width corresponding to the function condition becoming "false". (Ignored<br>for level-type signals.)<br>Unit: millisecond. Maximum: 32000.|
|Function|<br>|
|condition|**FAULT**<br>Printer fault.|
|(warning,error,||



**<mark>407</mark>** 

|control…)<br>**FAULT RIBBON**|Ribbon error is occurred.|
|---|---|
|**FAULT PAPER**|Paper empty or paper jam is occurred.|
|**FAULT CARRIAGE**|Carriage is open.|
|**FAULT MEMORY**|Out of memory is occurred.|
|**FAULT CUTTER**|Cutter error is occurred.|
|**FAULT OVERHEAT**|Stepping motor or print head is over heat.|
|**PAUSE**|Pause status is occurred.|
|**TAKELABEL**|Take label is occurred.|
|**IDLE**|Printer is idle.|
|**PRINT**|Printer is printing.|
|**FORWARD**|Paper is moving forward.|
|**BACKWARD**|Paper is moving backward.|
|**LOWLABEL**|Low label is occurred.<br>Since A209|
|**LOWRIBBON**|.<br>Low ribbon is occurred.|



###### **Example** 

**Sample code SET GPO1 HIGH,100,0,100,0,FAULT RIBBON SET GPO2 LOW,100,0,100,0,FAULT PAPER SET GPO3 POS,100,100,100,100,PAUSE SET GPO4 NEG,100,50,100,50,IDLE SET GPO1 LOW,0,0,0,0,FORWARD SET GPO2 LOW,0,0,0,0,BACKWARD** 

**<mark>408</mark>** 

**SET GPI** 

###### **Description** 

Use this command to receive the GPIO signals from external controlling devices. 

###### **Syntax** 

**SET GPIn signal, pulse, function** 

|**Parameter**<br>n|**Description**<br>n = 1 ~ 4<br>Four dedicated inputs are available for the desired control functions.|
|---|---|
|Signal state|**HIGH**<br>When a high level signal received, will activate the<br>following printer control functions.|
||**LOW**<br>When a low level signal received, will activate the<br>following printer control functions.|
||**POS**<br>When a positive pulse signal received, will activate the<br>following printer control functions.|
||**NEG**<br>When a negative pulse signal received, will activate the<br>following printer control functions.|
|Pulse|Filter pulse width. Ignored for level-type signals.<br>Unit: millisecond. Maximum: 32000.|
|Function||
|<br>|**PAUSE**<br>Toggle pause status.|
|(control)|**PAUSE ON**<br>Enter pause status.|
||**PAUSE OFF**<br>Cancel pause status.|
||**PRINT**<br>Print batch of labels.|
||**PRINT n**<br>n is numerical. Specify how many labels to print.<br>Maximum: 32000.|
||**CUT**<br>Cut immediately.|
||**FEED n**<br>n is numerical and the unit is dot. Specify the feeding<br>length. Maximum: 32000.|
||**BACKFEED n**<br>n is numerical and the unit is dot. Specify the<br>backfeeding length. Maximum: 32000.|
||**FORMFEED**<br>Feeding an empty label.|

**GPm** 

###### **Description** 

This command is used to get status of GPI, set status of GPO. (since A2.15.111) 

###### **Syntax** 

**GPm = n** 

|**Parameter**|**Description**|
|---|---|
|m|I, GPI signal|
||O, GPO signal|
|n|0, represents low lever of signal|
||1, represents high lever of signal|





###### **Example** 

**Sample code IF GPI2 = 1 THEN GPO1 = 0 GPO2 = 1 ELSE GPO1 = 1 GPO2 = 0 END IF**
